1993. This agreement must be put down in writing.
The difference is that the French plan envisages a
second stage, following the cease-fire, to hold talks
on implementing UN Security Council resolution No.
425, adopted way back in 1978, on the complete
withdrawal of Israeli forces from Lebanese territory.
Of course, Tel Aviv would prefer the Christopher
plan. Israel does not wish to pull out of southern
Lebanon, but at the same time wants to ensure the

by Hizballah’s “Katyushas.” Hizballah, and its backers
Syria and Iran, cannot accept this. Hizballah insists on
its right to strike against Israel for as long as it occupies

ning. More than 160 Lebanese civilians have been killed
and more than 200 have been injured already as 4 re-

civilian population there are insignificant — just a few

people have been injured. This prompts the thought that
Hizbaliah is more accurate in its target selection, striv-
ing not to shell civilian targets.

not deem it necessary to take Moscow's opinion into

efforts, preferring to deal only with the United States....

(FBIS Translated Text} During the past few years, in

to oppose Russia's intention to deliver nuclear technol-

I don’t think so. He added: On the whole, [words indis-
tinct) with Russia. The American President added that
the (?benefits) of the Moscow talks are much greater
than the existing differences of opinion between Russia
and America on nuclear [word indistinct] with Iran.

This statement by Bill Clinton was unexpected by
many because since March 1995 when the Russian-
Iranian agreement was signed, the White House has
been striving to put intense pressure on Moscow. [words
indistinct] that some representatives of Washington
even said that progress in Russian-American relations
was linked with Russia's refusal to carry out nuclear
cooperation with Iran.

As we know, Moscow was not affected by the pressure.
Moreover, as recently stated by the Russian Foreign
Ministry, a series of other joint projects on the peaceful
use of auclear energy are to be implemented.

(a course America is aware of all this. Therefore, what
has affected the American President's stand? America
is probably very well aware of the fact that the con-
struction of the Bushehr Nuclear Power Plant will not
create opportunities for Iran to use nuclear technology
for its military purposes. According to Georgiy Kaurov,
spokesman for the Russian Atomic Energy Ministry, an
atomic bomb cannot be built with some milligrams of
{word indistinct] plutonium isotopes in the light-water
reactors that are to be delivered to Bushehr—and this
is the type of reactors that are to be delivered to Iran.
Much larger quantities of this material are needed for
this purpose. Therefore. the Russian-Iranian agreement

cannot have any military consequences and will not en-

Russia: Primakov Criticizes U.S. for Acting
‘Single-Handedly’

LD2304194196 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1737 GMT 23 Apr 96

[From the “Diplomatic Panorama” feature]

[FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, Apr 23 (Interfax)
— Russian Foreign Minister Yevgeniy Primakov criti-
cized the USA for its attempts to “act single-handedly
in the efforts to settle the crisis on the border between
Lebanon and Israel.”

“Russia acts (Interfax’s note: in the Middle East) more
coordinatedly with France, and less with the USA; to a
greater extent with the European Union than with the
USA, unfortunately,” he told a group of journalists in
Moscow's Vnukovo-2 Airport upon his return from a
tour of the Middle East Monday night.

Nevertheless Primakov said in answer to a question
from Interfax that this does not involve differences
between Russia and the USA. "It so happened that the
USA acted paralielly, not in a pool. This has its positive
and negative points,” he explained.

“The Russian and American presidents agreed (Inter-
fax’s note: in Moscow) on coordinated actions,” Pri-
makov said, “and instructed their subordinates accord-
ingly.”

The road toward settling the crisis on the Lebanese-
Israeli border lies through compliance with Resolution

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copynght owners

FBIS-SOV-96-080
24 April 1996

No.425 on withdrawing Israeli troops from Lebanon,
issued by the U.N. security Council, he pointed out.
What matters is not only Hizballah, but also the fact that
part of Lebanon is under occupation, and this question
must be resolved in its essence, Primakov said.

According to him, “it will be possible to lay the entire
responsibility on the Lebanese government and on the
government of Syria, whose troops are also deployed in
Lebanon,” only when Israeli troops are withdrawn from
Lebanon.

“However no one will bear the entire responsibility for
Hizballah's actions till the time, when Israeli troops are
removed from Lebanese soil,” he said.

Therefore a major goal today is to stop the shellings
civilians fall victim to, Primakov said. "I think we
gained progress on these issues,” he added.

He categorically disagreed with the notion that either
Hizballah is destroyed, or Lebanon will continue to be
subjected to strikes. “This stand is not well-balanced,
not constructive, not paving the way for peace talks or
a peaceful solution to the Middle East crisis as a whole,
which is in a deadlock now,” Primakov pointed out.

According to him, "Russia categorically objects to the
shelling and bombing of Lebanon by Israel." “However
at the same time we object to the missile strikes
launched at Israel from Lebanon,” he said.

“The strikes hit largely civilian targets,” Primakov said.
In Lebanon only a few Hizballah fighters died as a result
of massive Israeli bombings; the remaining victims are
civilians.

“Therefore the physical destruction of Hizballah with
the help of such bombings is out of the question,”
Primakov pointed out.

“Russia continues its mission in the Middle East: Deputy
Foreign Minister Viktor Posuvalyuk remained in the
area; he will carry out shuttle trips,” he said.

With reference to the situation on the Lebanese-Israeli
border as a whole Primakov said “the crisis is very
serious, and it blocks further progress on the road toward
political solution to the Arab-Israeli conflict.”

Russia “will do all it can to resolve this crisis, leaning
on its long experience and extensive ties, and using its
potential and image it built for many years,” Primakov
said.

Russia “has been and will be present in the Middle
East,” Primakov pointed out. "We have our interests and
responsibilities there, and we shall act in the interests
of peace and stability.” he said.

RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS 23

According to him, the main thing for Russia is “not
political dividends, but the need to gain peace in the
area.” “We are interested in peace and stability there
maybe to an even greater extent than others,” Primakov
said.

The Middle East accepts Russia and wants it to step up
its efforts there, he stressed.

“We are appreciated as cochairman of the Madrid
conference. This is particularly noticeable in Arab
countries,” Primakov said. “Even Israel has no common
concept that only one power can do something, and is
interested in a constructive role played by Russia.”

During his Middle East tour lauuched on instructions
from President Boris Yeltsin Primakov visited Syria,
Lebanon and Israel, where he conferred with the leaders
of those countries.

In addition he coordinated his efforts with with his
French and Italian counterparts, Herve de Charette and
Susanna Agnelli, chair of the European Union.

He also conferred with Iran's Foreign Minister ‘Ali
Akbar Velayati and had several telephone conversations
with the U.S. secretary of State Warren Christopher,
Primakov said.

[Report by Sergey Postanogov under the “Lightning

(FBIS Translated Text] Whereas just a few days ago
the Lebanese mass media were going all out to criticize
Russia’s stance as regards the Israeli bomb raids, the
tone of their reports has undergone a sharp change since
Primakov’s visit.

Primarily because Russia, as a cosponsor of the peace
process, has clearly condemned Israel's military opera-
tions. During his meetings in Syria, Lebanon, and Israel
with these states’ leaderships, Primakov decisively ad-
vocated a cessation of combat actions. While acting in
coordination with other countries, Russia has nonethe-
less its own specific stance. It is distinguished primarily
by the fact that, in parallel with the immediate cessa-
tion of combat actions, it clearly notes the need to im-
plement UN Security Council resolution No. 425 which
provides for the withdrawal of Israeli forces from south-
ern Lebanon. The Americans and the French, for exam-
ple, are treating the implementation of this document as

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners

u RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS

something for the future and this, of course, does not
suit the Lebanese Government which is openly describ-
ing the Israeli occupation as the prime cause of military
actions.

This is what I was told by Yevgeniy Primakov prior to
his departure: “Russia intends to significantly galvanize
its role in the Near East." He noted that any decision
on the cessation of combat actions must be balanced,
without any advantages whatsoever for any of the sides
involved in the conflict.

Finding a compromise between warring sides is a highly
complex matter, and the minister reminded me that he
is “not a magician.” But there is no doubt about the
usefulness of his visit: This trip has inspired hope among
many people in the Near East that Russia will return to
the region and will play a weighty role here, just as it
did in the past.

Russia: ‘Countermeasures’ Threatened if NATO
Expands Into Baltics

WS2304140896 Tallinn BNS in English
1223 GMT 23 Apr 96

(FBIS Transcribed Text} Tallinn, Apr 23, BNS — The
Russian ambassador to Finland in an interview to the
Finnish Kalevala newspaper Tuesday [23 April] under-
lined Russia's opposition to NATO's expansion into the
Baltic states, threatening to take countermeasures.

“It is understandable the new independent Baltic states
want security like all other countries. But we cannot
agree with this being motivated by danger from Russia,”
Ambassador Yuriy Deryabin said in the interview. “In
the case of such fears there are other possibilities, for
example, the country’s own defence force, like that of
Finland.”

Deryabin said in the interview that if there are fears and
suspicions, there is also a sysiem of guarantees with a
number of options. The guarantees could be by NATO
or by Russia, or by both, he said.

“There can be a number of options, but I underline,
accession to a military alliance is not one of them,” the
Russian ambassador said. “.< is obvious that in such a
case the need to guarantee Russian security will take
priority. Some sort of adequate measures will have to
be taken.”

“Of course I exclude military operations against those
States. But if foreign military bases and nuclear arms
appear in the vicinity ~* our borders, we can only
imagine the sort of mxusures this compels us to take,”
Deryabin said.

The Russian ambassador warned that the Baltic states’
possible accession to NATO would cardinally change
the situation in the Baltic Sea area.

Deryabin said in his interview to the Finnish newspaper
that he could see no problem in the Baltic and East-

Euorpean states joining the European Union. “The EU
has a major role in the economic integration of Europe,
and we, too, would like to take part in it,” he said.

Russia: Yeltsin’s Baltic-Black Sea Nuclear-Free
Zone Offer Viewed

MK2304112396 Moscow NEZAVISIMAYA GAZETA
in Russian 23 Apr 96 p2

[Article by Russian Presidential National Security Aide
Yuriy Baturin: "Nuclear-Free Zone From the Baltic Sea
to the Black Sea"]

(FBIS Translated Text] The document “Russia's Stance
on Questions of Nuclear Safety,” introduced by the
Russian president at the Moscow nuclear safety and
security meeting on 20 April 1996, speaks about the
need to concentrate nuclear arms of the nuclear states
exclusively on their own territories. This proposal is
yet to be mulled over in the capitals of the other
nuclear states. It is obvious, however, that it contains
a big potential. It is in tune with the idea for reducing
the territory of “nuclear presence” and the many ideas
and understandings on ruling out the deployment of
nuclear arms in various regions: for example, Africa
and Southeast Asia, to say nothing about Latin America
or the South Pacific, where nuclear-free zones have long
been in existence.

A de-facto nuclear-free zone also took shape in South-
ern, Central, Eastern (including Ukraine and Belarus),
and Northern Europe (Baltic States) after Russia had
withdrawn from there its forces and operational-tactical
and tactical nuclear arms. Time has come to think about
giving it a legal status: in the shape of corresponding
commitments by the nuclear powers not to deploy their
nuclear arms in these regions.

We could speak about creating a nuclear-free zone from
the Baltic Sea to the Black Sea. This could involve
the three Baltic states, for Vyshegrad [as transcribed]
Group states, Belarus, Ukraine, Moldova, Romania, and
Bulgaria.

Thus, this could be a way out of the dead end in the
matter of creating a system of European security owing
to NATO's plans for eastward expansion. The creation
of such a zone could end Russia's worry over the
moving of nuclear arms closer to its borders and extend
security guarantees to countries in Southern, Central,
Eastern and Northern Europe.

The idea of a nuclear-free zone from the Baltic Sea
to the Black Sea is in tune with previous proposals, in
particular those made by Sweden for the creation of such
a zone in Central Europe under the social democratic
government of Olaf Palme. It is in order also to recall

This report may contain copynghted matenal. Copying and disseminaton
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners

26 RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS

the statement of the Belarusian foreign minister made
during the conference that examined the operation of
the Nonproliferation Treaty in April of 1995 on creating
a nuclear-free zone in Central Europe, which could be
also joined by Belarus and Ukraine.

The creation of a nuclear-free zone from the Baltic to
the Black Sea would be in line with Russia's national
interests and the interests of all European countries.
Such a zone would serve to consolidate the current
position, where this region is free of nuclear weaponry,
and would provide guarantees that it would not appear
there in the future. Of no less importance is the
circumstance that security guarantees — both “negative”
and “positive” — could be provided to these countries
not only by NATO but also by Russia. The zone idea
could be supplemented by the provisions of the so-
called “German formula” of 12 September 1990, under
which there should be neither nuclear weaponry nor its
delivery systems on the territory of the ex-GDR.

Given such conditions, of course, the joining of NATO
by some countries of Europe could have a purely
political nature because they would be provided with
NATO guarantees of security without the deployment
of nuclear weaponry and its delivery systems.

This would of course call for revising some of NATO's
fundamental documents, including the Washington
agreement of 1949, and would be in tune with the idea
to transform the military alliance into a political system
of collective security in Europe. It is precisely for this
reason that NATO's functionaries will not be overjoyed
at this proposal. It would be difficult to object to it,
however.

It would be also virtually impossible for those aspiring
for NATO membership to object. It is hard to imagine
that the leadership of, for example, the Czech Republic
and Poland would insist on having nuclear weapons

FBIS-SOV-96-080
24 April 1996

deployed on their territories. In this case they run the
risk of sparking the toughest protest of their peoples,
who would not like to become the target of Russian
nuclear missiles.

Russia: Police Search for Ex-Azerbaijani Premier,
Other Officials

LD2304163096 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1212 GMT 23 Apr 96

(FBIS Transcribed Text} MOSCOW, April 23 (Interfax)
— Russian police are trying to track down former
Azerbaijani Prime Minister Suret Guseynov and other
officials wanted by Baku.

The police have an extensive list of former Azerbaijani
politicians hiding in Russia who are to be detained and
extradited to Azerbaijan, ten of them of fairly high rank,
one police official told Interfax.

Asked if Guseynov was really hiding in Moscow, he
said “we would have arrested him a long time ago if he
were.”

Guseynov, who toppled former President Abulfaz
Elchibey in an army mutiny in summer 1993, is
accused by Baku of a failed coup attempt.

Under the bilateral extradition treaty, former Azerbaijani
Defense Chief Ragim Gaziyev, sentenced to death in
absentia on charges of treason by Baku, was arrested
and deported to Azerbaijan last week.

Ex-President Ayaz Mutalibov, who was detained at
the same time, is undergoing treatment at a Moscow
hospital for heart complaints. The Russian Prosecutor
General's Office is now examining the indictment
brought against Mutalibov before deciding whether or
not he should be extradited.

Russia: Yeltsin Meets With Residents of
Khabarovsk

LD2404082296 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian 0350 GMT 24 Apr 96

{By ITAR-TASS special correspondent
Shtorkh]

{FBIS Translated Text} Khabarovsk, 24 Apr (Itar-Tass)
— Russian President Boris Yeltsin started his working
day today with a walk round the streets of Khabarovsk
and visits to some of the stores in the city center. The
head of state went into a number of grocery stores
where he spent a long time talking to customers. The
main subjects of the conversations were payment of
pensions, the size of pensions, the standard of living
of the residents of Khabarovsk, the implementation of
“One's Own Home” program, the decree on which was
recently signed by the Russian president. In general,
prices in Khabarovsk are higher than in Moscow, while
the population receives wages similar to those common
to the whole of Russia. Boris Yeltsin noted that, no
matter what, Russia has now already overcome the most
difficult period and is entering the path of economic
growth.

Then the president visited the construction site of a
new wing of the rail bridge across the Amur river.
This bridge, which was built in 1913-1916, is a unique
construction. It is currently not just being reconstructed,
but a new bridge is being erected along side the one
being used at present. Railways Minister Gennadiy
Fadeyev noted in an interview with ITAR-TASS that
“with the commissioning of this bridge Russia will stand
before the ocean on two feet.” He said that they tried
to reconstruct the bridge back in the seventies, but they
were put off by the extent of the construction work, and
it was not until 1991-1992 that they found the required
sum of 3,100 billion rubles.

Boris Yeltsin talked to the bridge builders for almost
a whole hour. They asked him all kinds of questions,
including about the news of the death of Dzhokhar Du-
dayev. The Russian p:esident noted that the information
is being checked.

The president laid a wreath at the eternal flame of the
memorial to those from the Far East who perished in
World War II. While talking to veterans and students
who had come to the monument, he touched upon the
border problem, including the fate of the three disputed
islands which Russia and China have not been able to
agree upon for quite a long time now. The veterans
asked that Boris Yeltsin should try to keep these islands.
The head of state responded by promising to raise the

Andrey

problem in Russia’s interests.

[By Anatoliy Vostokov, Andrey Shtorkh]

{[FBIS Transcribed Text) KHABAROVSK, April 24
(Itar-Tass) — President Boris Yeltsin signed a decree
in Khabarovsk today, giving a presidential status to the
federal programme of economic and social development
of the Russian Far East and the Baykal region until the
year 2005.

A decision to raise the status of the Far East was adopted
taking into account an important role played by the Rus-
sian Far East and the Baykal region in Russia's so-
cial and economic revival and their geopolitical situa-
tion in the dynamically developing Asian-Pacific region,
the decree said. The federal programme of the socio-
economic development of the Far East and the Baykal
region for 1996-2005 was endorsed by the Russian gov-
ernment on April 15, 1996.

Yeltsin also signed a decree “On additional measures
for the rehabilitation of victims of political repressions.”
The document stipulates that appeals for rehabilitat.on
made by citizens who in childhood stayed with their par-
ents in detention centers or were in exile or compounds
for resettled people will be considered in a period of
time stipulated by the Russian law “On rehabilitation of
victims of political repressions.”

The government of the Russian Federation was in-
structed to look into the problem of allocating addi-
tional funds needed for the realization of this law and
the above mentioned decree and also submit proposals
on amendments and addenda to the federal legislature.

Suffice it to say that this edict has eased the burden

not take a penny from indebted agricultural producers.
And electricity for production needs will be half-price
for agricultural enterprises. Irrespective of their form
of ownership, all farms — be they a collective farm
untouched by the reforms or an association of private
farms, where land and property have been privatized —
have been looked at by the president.

The luckiest ones, nevertheless, or those in the vanguard
of the reforms in the countryside — the private farmers.
Judging by the edict, they are becoming the favorites
of the president and the government. Recently, imme-
diately after this edict was signed, the private farmers
organization AKKOR [Association of Russian Peasant
(Private) Farms and Agricultural Cooperatives} and the
Union of Landowners held a joint meeting and stated
that they will support Boris Yeltsin at the June elections.

The president has set the task of formulating a federal
program (followed by regional ones) to support private
farming by | August this year. The point is to provide
farmers with equipment, if not at the level of collective
and state farms, at least at the rate of one plowing
tractor per 100 hectares and one grain harvesting
combine and one truck per 150 hectares of grain crops.
Nowadays, without even having the pool of equipment
available to collective farms, private farmers are solidly
outperforming them in terms of yields, profitability, and
prevention of after-harvesting crop loss. Together with
personal plots they produced more than half of Russia's
gross food outpe: in 1995. It is time to strengthen
their material infrastructure. It will also be developed
through the use of machine-tractor stations. The land
tax deferment granted to private farmers by the law will
be extended to more than five years from the moment
they create a farm.

Yet another chronic “pain” — the absurd system of
taxation for private farmers — has not been forgotien
cither. Sometimes, peasants joke, a ton of potatoes
accounts for a ton of paper. The president has told
organs of power that, within the limits of their powers,
they should simplify private farm record keeping and
abolish or reduce taxes for the first and most difficult
part of their existence. And to put an end to the
phenomenon of private farmers having too little land
(a farm in our country averages slightly more than 40
hectares, whereas the figure in Germany is arouad 100).
the president has decreed that since one-man farms do

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 29

not yet have the resources to expand their holdings
by buying parcels of land, 800 billion rubles is to be
channeled into funding credit for private farmers and
other agricultural producers to buy land.

The president proposes that next year at the latest there
should be a return to soft loans for agricultural producers
— albeit at 25 percent annual interest — so long as they
cannot afford market rates. A special federal fund will
be created. The president intends to resolutely remove
an old yoke from agricultural producers’ shoulders. |
am referring to the social sphere. Hitherto the process
of transferring housing, garden plots, and the like to
local administrations’ budgets has been sluggish. By his
decree the president has set a specific date by which the
handover must be finally completed — | January 1997.
Facilities which have not been transferred will have to
be maintained by the local authorities in conjunction
with the agricultural enterprises.

Realizing the extent to which this step by the president
toward resolving chronic rural problems could boost
his rating among the peasants, and particularly the
private farmers, who have long been playing the role
of a catalyst of reform in the countryside now, the
Communists and Agrarians expedited the adoption of
the Land Code. The anti-market draft was approved by
a majority of votes on its second reading literally the
day after the presidential edict was signed. AKKOR and
the Russian Union of Landowners have reques.cd Boris
Yeltsin not to sign this document since it curtails the
constitutional rights of private landowners. Under the
adopted draft, areas of land designated for agricultural
purposes cannot be sold, bought, or mortgaged. Such
market operations are allowed only for gardens, dacha
plots, and personal plots. The transfer of land shares is
terminated. They cannot be gifted, sold, or bequeathed.
This is a colossal blow to owners of land shares,
particularly for pensioners whose land share is a means
of survival.

“The Communists, who voted unanimously for the
Graft, are contradicting their own program,” was State
Duma deputy and AKKOR President Viadimir Bas-
machnikov’s comment on this event. “In their program
they advocate that land should belong to those who work
it. But thanks to this version of the Land Code it will
belong to the collective farm generals. Because a land
share will only be transferable to the incorporation cap-
ital of the joint-stock company — that is, the collective
farm.”

Today Samara farmer Matveyev leases land shares from
his fellow villagers and nobody calls him a landowner
Everyone only thanks him. For conscientiously paying

is report may contain copynghted matenal. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copynght owners

%” RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

a substantial leasing payment and using the leased areas
in a way the local collective farm should learn from.

But the directors and chairmen don't like this. The
people articulating their interests in the Duma — the
Communists, Agrarians, and Zhirinovskiy supporters (it
was by their votes that the Land Code was adopted)
— are undoubtedly hoping to win the deracinated
rural electorate over to their side in the course of the
presidential elections. The high-speed adoption of the
Code is a pre-election game. The Russian president
will not sign such a version of this document, which
contradicts the Constitution, Vice Premier Aleksandr
Zaveryukha assured us. The other rural voters — owners
and proprietors — are getting to stand on their own feet
and are increasing in number.

Russia: Zyuganov Approves Results of Moscow
Nuclear Summit

LD2304145096 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1212 GMT 23 Apr 96

(FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, April 23 (Interfax)
— Communist leader Gennadiy Zyuganov approved of
the recent summit on nuclear safety.

“The planet is obviously overloaded with radioactive

materials, and their spread is fraught with deadly danger
for mankind,” he told a Tuesday press conference,
adding that “attempts to take radioactive waste out of
Russia will be strengthened.”

“The irresponsibility that has seeped into all pores of
Russian society has worked its way into the nuclear
safety sphere as well,” he said.

As for the decision on Chernobyl's future, “that is
wholly Ukraine's, and should be weil thought-out, as it
will need enormous financial expenditures,” Zyuganov
said, adding that “even bodies of Chernobyl victims
buried not at two but at six meters continue to emit
death.”

Zyuganov expressed skepticism toward polls show-
ing rises in President Boris Yeltsin's rating. “The
democrats’ [rating] always rises sharply on the eve of
the elections, but after the elections, for some reason, it
always falls by the same amount,” he said.

In answer to an Interfax question on Yeltsin's declara-
tion that he is confident of victory, Zyuganov said “it is
the people who should have confidence above all, but
unfortunately, they have lost it.” Zyuganov promised
that if he were elected, he would be guided not by the
Communist Party program, but by his pre-election pro-
gram, which was “formed by a wide coalition.”

Russia: Officials Told To Answer ‘Hot-Line’ Calls
in Person

MM2304155096 Moscow IZVESTIYA in Russian
23 Apr 96 pl

[Report by Vasiliy Kononenko: “Viktor Chernomyrdin
Has Told Officials To Answer Calls Personally” |

(FBIS Translated Text] A move in the direction of com-
mon sense — this is how it is possible to describe the
directive from Head of Government V. Chernomyrdin
whereby he charges officials with responding to tele-
phone calls made over the government communications
network. This document stipulates that it is permitted to
transfer “hot lines” to reception, i.e. to secretaries, only
if the subscriber is temporarily absent.

In recent years the owners of “hot lines’ —- ATS-
1 [Automatic Telephone Exchange] and ATS-2 —
had themselves rendered this protected communications
system ineffective. The receiver is invariably lifted by
secretaries, whose chief official duty, as practice shows,
is now that of protecting the chief from outsiders, not
to mention journalists.

Top-ranking state officials found a way round the situ-
ation. They provided themselves with super-protected
sets marked "SK" — which is intended to indicate
"Kremlin link.” But the number of subscribers to this
network quickly increased to 100, so then the Federal
Government Communications and Information Agency
received a new order to create a very restricted commu-
nications system — only for the chosen few. Soon an
apparatus appeared in the offices of top leaders and pres-
idential aides, which they called the “balalaika.” The in-
structions for it state that secretaries are forbidden to lift

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners

32 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

the receiver even if the “boss” is not in the room. Orig-
inally there were 24 “chosen” officials. But this closed
telephone network is not capable of ensuring the run-
ning of the state. Therefore the premier was obliged
to issue the directive, which puts a stop to bad habits
among officials.

ation in the second round of the presidential election,

explicit politicians.”

Zyuganov said the economic policy of the future gov-
ernment had been discussed, among others, with Sergey
Glazyev, the leader of the National Committee of the
Democratic Party of Russia.

Without laying out the policy, Zyuganov said the
Agrarian Party's programme was likely to make a bulk
of the future agrarian policy.

Zyuganov said “retention of all forms of ownership, a
sufficiently broad freedom with support the state” is his
creed where agricultural producers are concerned.

Media Issues

Russia: Yegorov Speech at Launch of News Agency
MM2904101796 Moscow ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI
("Political Wednesday” section) in Russian 24 Apr 96
p iii

{Report by Nikita Vaynonen: “Regional Press Given
Access to Information on the Authorities’ Activity"]

{[FBIS Translated Text] There were 1,078 television and
radio broadcasting licenses issued in Russia by mid-
March 1996. By today, according to reports from the
All-Russia Television and Radio Broadcasting Service,
this number is already approaching 1,100. It is, however,
paradoxical: The number of broadcasters has increased
mainly thanks to local television and radio broadcasting
companies — private, rather than state-owned, more-
over — and now we have 10 nonstate channels for every
State-owned one, while the population of Russia, exclud-
ing the capitals and major cities, is tuning in primarily
to local airwaves. This abnormality should be put right
by the Regional Press Agency, which has been set up
through the efforts of the Russian Federation Presiden-
tial Staff on the initiative of journalists from Federation
components, and mainly by its Information Administra-
tion. This a truly unprecedented step: The regional press
is given direct access to information about the activity
of central authorities, and it will be possible to acquire
such information not only from documentary sources
but also first-hand from the top officers of state, minis-
ters, eminent politicians and scientists, program drafters,
and so on.

Addressing local journalists at the agency's launch,
Nikolay Yegorov, head of the Russian Federation Pres-
idential Staff, emphasized that the current orientation of
political life in its entirety toward the forthcoming pres-
idential election is a new and unusual phenomenon for
Russia. For the first time in its entire history, the coun-
try will be freely electing the head of state. In Soviet
times it was said that we had elections but no choice.
Now the situation is diametrically opposite. This will
not be a choice between individuals. It has so happened
that the coming to power of a leader who will head the
State is linked with the choice of a social and economic
system. It has so happened that, on the threshold of the
third millennium, Russia is again facing the choice: To
be or not to be.

Enjoying a tremendous potential, N. Yegorov said, our
country has embarked on radical economic and political
transformations. A considerable part of the distance

has already been covered. Of course, mistakes and

portant, task will be to promote information exchanges
between the center and the regions, to bring them closer

together, and to ensure mutual understanding between

When regional and local authorities are finally formed

Democratic Party of Russia leader is unlikely to get as

Government House with the council of the Russian
center for legal Aid to war veterans and invalids. He said

Difficult Conditions’

MM2304140596 Moscow TRUD in Russian
23 Apr 96 p2

[Report by TRUD military observer Samail Temitbiyev
"Spring Draft")

([FBIS Translated Text] The latest draft to the Army
is proceeding in very difficult conditions: Economic
disorder, a complex political situation, and “hot spots,
the main one being Chechnya. Alli this has an effect on
the mood of the draftees and their families and friends,
and it raises a multitude of questions.

In spring of this year, 200,000 men are to be drafted to
the Army. In all, 65 percent of these will be drafted to
the Armed Forces, and the rest to the Ministry of In-
ternal Affairs Internal Troops, the Railroad Troops, the
Federal Government Communications and Information
Agency, and the Federal Border Service.... Period of
service — two years. Only men on contract will serve
in the “hot spots.” According to information I received
from the General Staff, 15 percent of draftees are not fit
for service for health reasons, another 15 percent have
received a one-year deferment for treatment. These fig-
ures cannot but generate concern: One drafitee in three
does not meet the requirements set by the medical com-
mission.

The following facts also make you think. A total of
27 percent of servicemen in the fall draft were brought

figures scream at you.

In 1995 there were 30,000 “dodgers” in the Army —
that is, those who tried to avoid service on one pretext
or another. In all 59 men were convicted, and criminal
proceedings were instituted against 114.

Incidentally, the Moscow Government has examined
the question of increasing the administrative liability
of individuals who try to dodge military service. The
relevant draft law is now at the City Duma. It envisages
the sharp tightening up of fines imposed on “dodgers”:
Whereas before the fines did not exceed one-half of the
minimum wage, once the law is adopted they will be
equivalent to between 10 and 50 minimum wages.

Russia: Proton Rocket To Put Last Mir Station
Component Into Orbit

LD2304132696 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian 1250 GMT 23 Apr 96

{By ITAR-TASS correspondent
nenkova]

Veronika Roma-

([FBIS Translated Text] Moscow, 23 Apr (Itar-Tass)
— A Russian Proton heavy booster rocket blasted
off successfully today at 1548 Moscow time [1148
GMT] from launch pad 81 at the Baykonur space
facility. It is to put into orbit the new research module
Priroda [Nature], which will be the final component
of the Mir space station. ITAR-TASS correspondents
were told this at the press center of the Military
Space Forces of the Russian Federation. [Moscow
ITAR-TASS in English at 1256 GMT, in an item by
Veronika Romanenkova «4 datelined Moscow, adds
the following: “The Priroda module which will dock to
the Mir station on April 26, will be the last component
of the Russian orbital complex. The main unit of
the Mir station started working in space a decade
ago. Four modules — Kristall, Kvant, Kvant-2 and
Spektr — were attached to it during these years. “The
space construction project" will be completed soon.
Priroda is packed with great quantities of Russian-made
instrumentation: optical systems for investigations of
the Earth's natural resources. They will be used even
by the present inhabitants of Mir, Russian cosmonauts
Yuriy Onufrienko and Yuriy Usachev. The new module
carries also a cargo for the third crew member, U.S.
woman astronaut Shennon Lucid. NASA sent scientific
equipment for her.

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 35

("The Russian side hurried with the launching of Priroda
precisely due to the American research programme
which should be carried out by Lucid. The lift-off was
initially scheduled for April 26. It was also decided ‘to
accelerate’ the docking with the station. New modules
were usually ‘to tether’ to Mir on the ninth day after the
blast-off. It is now planned to carry out this operation
in three days. The Priroda was successfully placed in
near earth orbit and is to dock with the Mir on Friday
afternoon.]

Federal Assembly

Russia: Upper House Notes ‘Extremely
Unsatisfactory’ Budget Figures

MM2304152396 Moscow ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
in Russian 24 Apr 96 p5

(Unattributed report under the “In the Federation Coun-
cil” rubric: "Budget Deficit's Dangerous Consequences”
— from the “Documents” section]

([FBIS Translated Text] Having heard and discussed the
Russian Federation Government's report “On Progress
in the Implementation of the Federal Law “On the 1996
Federal Budget,’” the Federation Council has adopted a
decree noting that the situation as regards the budget's
implementation is extremely unsatisfactory. The federal
budget’s tax revenues during the first quarter of the
current year amounted to only 56 percent of the planned
figure. Federal budget expenditures have been financed
only to the 71 percent level, including 59 percent for the
national economy, and 66 percent for social and cultural
measures. The budget deficit has increased by a factor
of 1.5.

The budgetary policy pursued in the coumitry is not
geared to using the methods of state incentives for
the development of society's productive potential. The
absence of coordination in the work of tax, customs,
and financial organs in forming the revenue section of
budgets at all levels is having a negative effect. One
of the reasons for the increase of nonpayments into the
federal budget is the breach of payments discipline by
individual enterprises and organizations. Mechanisms
for uniform state financial monitoring by the organs of
executive power to increase the revenue base of budgets
at all levels have not been legislatively enshrined. There
is NO proper monitoring of the targeted utilization of
budget funds.

The flaws in the budget process and direct breaches
of current procedures for the formation and utilization
of budgetary funds are exacerbating social tension in
individual regions and in a series of national economic
sectors.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners

%6 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

The Federation Council has drawn the Russian Govern-
ment’s attention to the extremely unsatisfactory imple-
mentation of the federal law "On the 1996 Federal Bud-
get” and to the considerable delay in submitting to the
State Duma the quarterly distribution of 1996 budget
revenues and expenditures, the list of federal targeted
programs, and the distribution of appropriations for the
maintenance of the Armed Forces. The government has
been instructed to take urgent measures to ensure the
implementation of the federal budget’s revenue section.
The Ministry of Finance has been requested to submit
a schedule for the repayment of debts relating to trans-
fers of financial support for Russian components from
the Federal Fund, bring order to the system for the fed-
eral budget’s implementation in cash terms, and impose
tight controls over the targeted utilization of budgetary
funds.

Russia: Grachev Answers Deputies on Chechnya
Ambush

MM2404090396 Moscow ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI
in Russian 24 Apr 96 pp I, iii

(Unattributed report: “Grachev in the Duma: Conjec-
tures and Documentary Evidence” — Report begins on
page | and continues on page iii of “Political Wednes-
day” section]

{FBIS Translated Excerpt] Defense Minister Pavel
Grachev's name is once again the focus of sensational-
ist reporting. This was prompted by his 19 April speech
to State Duma deputies during the debate on the tragic
events near the village of Yaryshmarda in Chechnya —
the ambush and shooting of a convoy of federal forces
by Dudayevites.

Commenting on Pavel Grachev's speech at the State
Duma, Yevgeriy Kiselev, anchorman of NTV's “Itogi"
program, described it in his Sunday [21 April] statement
as “prompting legitimate doubts about the defense
minister's loyalty to his supreme commander in chief.”
Some other mass media have made statements along
similar lines.

ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI has decided to let the documen-
tary evidence speak for itself. We are publishing frag-
ments from the conversation which took piace at the
Duma between the legislators and Russia's defense min-
ister. Readers can draw their own conclusions. [passage
omitted]

Oleg Morozov: I have the following question to ask....
Being a nonmilitary person, I fail to understand this:
How did it happen that a convoy was ambushed by 200
gunmen? After all, we are talking about 200 men, not
just three or five, that is a very large group! No surveil-
lance at all. My specific question is: Does the blame

FBIS-SOV-96-080
24 April 1996

for each specific incident rest with specific third-rate
— if you would pardon the expression — majors and
colonels? Or should we be looking from a completely
different angle at everything that is happening in Chech-
nya, at the military aspects of operations?

Pavel Grachev: I do not wish to say that the majors,
colonels, and other people directly in charge of combat
actions are third-rate. As a rule, they are good com-
manders who have gained experience in Afghanistan
and have been fighting in Chechnya for many months
already. But the opposite side is not sitting on its hands,
either. The Dudayevites even enjoy a certain advantage
as regards, for example, their local knowledge. This is
quite important, because this is war.

To continue. It is not a particularly difficult task to
deploy 200 men on both sides of a ravine within firearm
sniping range. This is what we call “ambush action.”
Once again, the bandits used the local terrain, they used
enough greenery for camouflage, as well as the fact that
the aviation was grounded at the airfield. One of the
command’s mistakes was the fact that there were no
helicopters overflying the convoy’s route, but if they
had done so this would have been a troops operation.
The military know this. There are also other reasons
for the poor organization of collaboration between the
commanders of the two regiments which resulted ia
such losses.

1212 GMT 23 Apr 96

[By Pavel Kuznetsov]

(FBIS Trarscribed Text) MOSCOW, April 23 (ITAR-
TASS) — The State Duma (Russian parliament lower
house) is now drafting a decision on amnesty to
participants in Chechen illegal armed detachments,
provided for by the plan of the Russian leadership
on a peaceful settlement in Chechnya, Duma speaker
Gennadiy Seleznev told reporters here on Tuesday.

He specified that amnesty will be granted only to
gunmen “who are not terrorists and did not commit
grave crimes”.

Seleznev also noted that the Duma intends to debate
the Russian president's peace initiatives on Chechnya
in May, since there are no mechanisms now how this
will be done.

It is not clear either who will speak at the Duma:
premier Viktor Chernomyrdin, head of the Chechen
Republic Doku Zavgayev or any member of the State

Russia: Dudayev Said Killed in Rocket Attack on
Chechen Village

LD2304162296 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian 1537 GMT 23 Apr 96

{By ITAR-TASS correspondent Sharip Asayev]

(FBIS Translated Text] Groznyy, 23 Apr (Itar-Tass) —
Dzhokhar Dudayev died in a rocket attack carried out
by federal forces on the immediate surroundings of the
village of Gekhi-Chu on the evening of 21 April. A
source in “the government of Ichkeria” told ITAR-TASS
that Dzhokhar Dudayev arrived at this village, 35 km
southwest of Groznyy, with his wife Alevtina Fedorovna
and was staying with his personal bodyguards at the
house of prosecutor Magomed Dzhaniyev. .

On the evening of 21 April, a satellite linkup was
planned with a mediator to discuss the terms of negoti-
ations proposed by Moscow. Dzhokhar Dudayev drove
Out into open country with his aide Dukvakh Ibragimov,
while his wife remained at the house. It was then that the
rocket attack occurred. Dudayev was seriously wounded
and died the same evening, the source reported.

The funeral is to be held in the village of Shalazhi, 5
km southwest of Gekhi-Chu.

Russia: Maskhadov Said Likely To Be Chechens’
‘Real Leader’

LD2404110996 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1048 GMT 24 Apr 96

{By Tamara Zamyatina]

{FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW April 24 (Itar-Tass)
— The death of Chechen separatist leader Dzhokhar
Dudayev may radically and positively promote the
peaceful Chechen settlement as the most influential
man in Chechnya is now separatist chief of staff Aslan
Maskhadov, who is well-known and respected by many
politicians in Moscow.

Although officially Dudayev’s powers have been trans-
ferred to his deputy Zelimkhan Yandarbiyev, the real
leader of Ichkeria, as the separatists call Chechnya, is
Maskhadov who is widely respected in the rebel repub-
lic and is well-known to the Russian leadership as he
was the chief peace negotiator last summer.

Presidential legal aide Mikhail Krasnov, who also
participated in the last summer peace talks in Groznyy,
totd Tass on Wednesday that although Yandarbiyev is
“a grey Cardinal” in Dudayev’s entourage, his influence
does not spread further than the so-called state council
of Ichkeria.

During the summer negotiations the name of Yandar-
biyevy was not even mentioned either officially or in
private talks, according to Krasnov who said that Yan-
darbiyev has no real power in his hands in contrast to
Maskhadov.

FBIS-SOV -96-080
24 April 1996

"Some time ago we had (Konstantin) Chernenko as
the General Secretary of the central committee (of the
CPSU), but he was not the real leader", Krasnov said
referring to ailing Soviet communist leader who was
replaced by Mikhail Gorbachev in 1985.

Maskhadov has all chances to become the real leader
now. Russian Interior minister Anatoliy Kulikov, Na-
tionalities minister Vyacheslav Mikhaylov have both re-
cently told Tass that they would prefer to negotiate with
Maskhadov. The same opinion is shared by Arkadiy
Volskiy, the number 2 man at last year talks.

All the Russian negotiators agreed that Maskhadov was
a staunch supporter of a peaceful settlement of the crisis
and it was only the opposition from Dudayev which
did not allow him to fulfil the military agreement with
Moscow signed on July 31, 1995.

Maskhadov did not have power enough to neutralise the
opposition of Dudayev as he could have been killed for
disobeyance.

Now Maskhadov can show himself as an independent
leader of Chechnya as he enjoys support of field
commanders and of the local population who often
rallied with portraits of Dudayev only for fear of
punishment.

It is not surprising that popular Chechen chieftain
Shamil Basayev opposed the transfer of powers to
Yandarbiyev and agreed only that he governs until new
elections.

All Tass correspondents who worked in Chechnya can
confirm that Maskhadov is not only a professional
military man, but also a good speaker who can convince
his chieftains and the fighters in the necessity to achieve
peace.

Russia: ITAR-TASS Carries Dudayev Obituary

LD2304171996 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1629 GMT 23 Apr 96

{(FBIS Transcribed Text} MOSCOW, Apr 23 (Itar-Tass)
— Dzhokhar Musayevich Dudayev was born on 15 May
1944 in the village if Pervomayskoye of the Chechen-
Ingush Autonomous republic.

The Dudayevs, together with other Chechen families.
were soon exiled to Kazakhstan on order from Joseph
Stalin. Dzhokhar Dudayev returned to Northern Cauca-
sus in 1957.

He learned to fly at the Tambov higher pilot-training
school and later graduated from the Gagarin Air Force
Academy as an air force pilot and engincer first class
He retired from active service in 1990 in the rank of
majort-general.

This report may contain copynghted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV -96-086
24 April 1996

From 1987-1990, Dudayev served as commander of a
division of heavy bombers stationed in Estonia and as
chief of the Tartu garrison. He was known to Estonians
as a “rebellious general” after he refused to obey
the order to block Estonia’s parliament and television
centre.

In 1990, Dudayev was transferred from Tartu to
Groznyy, where he resigned from active service. He
was offered high posts in the air force, including that
of the commander of an airforce army, but he declined,
saying: “For me, being a rank-and-file Chechen is the
highest post.”

On August 19, 1991 during the first hours of the botched
coup in Moscow, Dudayev took the side of the Russian
parliament and Russian President Boris Yeltsin. He next
demanded independence for Chechnya.

The Presidium of the Supreme Soviet of the RSFSR
condemned Dudayev's claim in its resolution dated 10
October 1991. Groznyy responded by calling parliamen-
tary and presidential elections in the Chechen republic.
Dudayev was elected President of Chechnya and pro-
claimed his republic “independent.”

In December 1994, Russia sent troops into Chechnya in
a bid to protect the territorial integrity of the Russian
Federation and keep the Chechen republic within the
Federation.

Dudayev was reported killed during a missile attack on
the village of Gekhi-Chu from April 21-22.

People who served with Dudayev said he was a tough,
harsh and hot-tempered man. He used to be rebuked for
authoritarianism and love of power. He later learned to
keep his emotions in check and look unperturbed in any
situation.

Dudayev was a member of the Communist Party of the
Soviet Union from 1968 to 1991, when the party was
dissolved. He never left the party formally.

His wife Alevtina is a fashion designer. His son Aviar
was born in 1971, his daughter Dana was born in 1973,
and his younger son Dega was born in 1983.

Dudayev loved music and poetry, particularly that writ-
ten by 19th century Russian poets Mikhail Lermontov
and Aleksandr Pushkin. He also enjoyed Russian prose
writers Leo Tolstoy and Anton Chekhov.

Dudayev played many sports and won distinction in
wrestling, gymnastics, tennis and karate.

Russia: Deputy Borovoy Interviewed on Impact of
Dudayev’s Death

LD2304214596 Moscow NTV in Russian

1535 GMT 23 Apr 96

{(FBIS Report] This station in its “Hero of the Day”

conducted by anchorman Pavel Lobkov. The interview
deals with the situation around reports of Dudayev's
death.

Asked whether denials of Dudayev's death can be
believed, Borovoy says: “I think that these denials
are connected with the fact that a very dangerous
situation is arising in Chechnya because if, it turns
out that the special services did that, there may be
retaliations. If it turns out to be true [changes thought],
the cunsequences are very dangerous because if nobody
manages to concentrate power in Chechnya now —
and you understand there are field commanders, very
independent people — that structure of resistance may
break down into many independent structures, forces,
groups. And a Hizballah-siyle situation will arise in
which these groups act individually, not in a coordinated
manner. In any event considerably more people will
need to be assembled for talks than now. Not to beat
about the bush, if three forces could hold talks now,
unless a leader arises, should Dudayev be dead, of
course, the situation will grow much more complicated.
To say nothing of the fact that retaliatory measures have
been provided for, and Dudayev himself spoke of that,
should there be an attack on him. That unfortunately is
the most dangerous version.”

Asked to name possible Dudayev successors, Borovoy
says: “Basayev has the greatest authority in society
today, but he is the least acceptable figure to Moscow
for talks. Therefore | am very much counting on him
not being put forward as leader.”

“There is also Aslan Maskhadov,” Lobkov says.
Borovoy replies: “Quite right! There is another figure
who is acceptable to Moscow, for instance, Usman
Imayev, who recently appeared in Chechnya, and many
participants in previous talks said that he was the most
acceptable figure.”

Lobkov says: “So there is Imayav who began the talks
but was then removed by Dudayev, and Maskhadov
who was then appointed in Imayev's place?” Boroboy

side, too.”

either the gunmen or the greater part of Chechnya’s

[FBIS Translated Excerpt} [passage omitted] Many have

away somewhiere in the West, marked: “To be opened
in the event of my death.” In this context it cannot be
ruled out that in the very near future some sensational
leaks could occur. Under another theory, it was “po-
litically inexpedient” to eliminate Dudayev, because he
effectively controlled the gunmen, and therefore nego-
tiations with him were inevitable. Yet another theory is
connected with the name of Pavel Grachev. His state-
ment in the Duma last Friday appeared dubious to many,
while Yevgeniy Kiselev in his ltogi TV program went
as far as to suggest that Grachev “has betrayed the pres-
ident.” Maybe Grachev, who earlier also demonstrated
his intransigence toward Dudayev, decided to cut “the
Gordian knot” of Chechnya. It was not an accident that

lowed to finish off the gunmen. Maybe this was what
was in effect done, when Dudayev went into an open

FBIS-SOV -96-080
24 April 1996

field (under one theory) to negotiate with Moscow's go-
between through a satellite communications system. It
was a matter of technology to track him. We are not
making statements; we are only making suppositions.
Dudayev could also have been killed by accident. But
the theory that Moscow finally signed a death verdict
on the general is backed up by Dudayev’'s extensive
interview with INTERFAX over the weekend. It was
irreconcilable in form and content: Negotiations only
with Yeltsin, without any mediators; and independence

and then it transpired that the Georgian president, who
had become inconvenient to many of his own associates,
was killed. Another mysterious death of another Cau-
casian, Stalin, should also be recalled here. His loyal ser-
vant Beria was by his bedside day and night. He was so
loyal that he did not even give the doctors access to the
sick man. Yesterday Yarikhanov announced a state of

lazhi. The “funeral commission” will apparently also be
headed by Yarikhanov. Under Soviet tradition, the head
of a commission for the funeral of a general secretary
himself becomes the next general secretary.

What next? Paradoxical as it might be, two mutually
exclusive scenarios were born in Strasbourg, where
a Russian Duma delegation is attending a session of
the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe
[PACE]. PACE President Leni Fisher [name as translit-
erated) learned of Dudayev’s purported death from a
KOMMERSANT-DAILY correspondent literally out in

consequences will be terrible.” The Russian delegation,
quite the contrary, was swayed by a different opinion:

lies in between. To be sure, there will be those who
will try to organize a kind of “jihad” across Russia. In-
fighting might begin among the field commanders. But
many of them might also reaiize that without the irrec-
oncilable Dudayev around it would be more reasonable
for them to come to terms with Moscow. If leadership
is Claimed by Yariknanov, he has some experience with
such negotiations. True, yet another assumption could
also be made: Maybe Dudayev is alive? His lookalike
will be hastily buned, while the head of the separatists

himself will take shelter in a Muslim country under the
protection of some royal grace. And then legends about
Dudayev will be going around Chechnya as they are

much....

Russia: ‘Realistic Steps’ on Chechnya Plan ‘Still
Not Taken’

MM2404085196 Moscow IZVESTIYA in Russian
24 Apr 96 p2

{Report by Igor Rotar: “Realistic Steps Toward Peace in
Chechnya Have Still Not Been Taken, Republic Chief
Said to IZVESTIYA in Telephone Conversation")

(FBIS Translated Text} On 23 April an attempt
was made in Groznyy on the life of Badruddi
Dzhamalkhanov, Chechen Republic Government vice
premier. The vice premier received a spinal injury but
two of his escorts were killed. A Chechen Republic
representative under the Russian Federation president,
Isa Aliroyev, informed IZVESTIYA that Badruddi
Dzhamalkhanov was traveling to work early in the
morning when someone suddenly opened up with an
automatic weapon from a car approaching from the
opposite direction. Dzhamalkhanov received attention
then left the hospital.

Until 1991 Dzhamalkhanov worked as Shalinskiy
Rayon Soviet Executive Committee chairman, but
immediately after Dzhokhar Dudayev came to power
he proved to be one of the most active opponents of
the Chechén Republic’s first president. Together with
Provisional Council Chairman Umar Avturkhanov he
formed the anti-Dudayev opposition’s armed detach-
ments in the village of Znamenskoye. After Groznyy
was taken by Russian forces he worked as minister for
affairs of civil defense, emergency situations, and the
elimination of natural disasters in the government of
Salambek Khadzhiyev.

“Badruddi Dzhamalkhanov is one of the most prominent
members of the opposition to the Dudayev regime, and
we can therefore have no doubt as to who were the
perpetrators of this attempt on his life. This is certainly
not the first attempt on lives of the Chechen Republic's
leadership — as a matter of fact, Dudayev's men really
hunt these people down,” Isa Aliroyev considers.

The situation in Chechnya remains extremely tense.
The Russian Federation Territorial Administration press
center in Groznyy reported to IZVESTIYA that the
most unstable location in the republic is still the rayon
center of Shali, situated 40 km from Groznyy. Federal
forces are continuing to blockade this population center.
In conversation with your IZVESTIYA correspondent,
Aliroyev did not exclude the possibility of federal troops
launching an assault on the rayon center. In Aliroyev's
opinion, the combat operations here did not wind down
after the public announcement of Boris Yeltsin's plan
for a peaceful settlement of the situation in the Chechen
Republic.

FBIS-SOV-96-080
24 April 1996

“It is first and foremost the gunmen who are, of
course, causing the war to continue through their
provocations, but the federal forces willingly give in to
these provocations. You should not respond to a burst
of automatic fire by opening up with mortars,” Aliroyev
considers.

In essence, the Chechen Republic chief also indirectly
agrees with Aliroyev’s assessment. In a conversation
with your IZVESTIYA correspondent, a very cautious
Doku Zavgayev admitted that realistic steps to imple-
ment the president's plan for a settlement have not yet
been taken.

Meantime, the latest session of the State Commission for
a Settlement in the Chechen Republic took place under
the chairmanship of Viktor Chernomyrdin in Moscow
23 April. ITAR-TASS reports that the session examined
a range of questions concerning the “organization of
consultations and negotiations with the separatists with a
simultaneous cessation of terrorist acts by the gunmen.”
Commission members continued to discuss missions
“connected with the redeployment of federal forces and
the plan to withdraw them from the republic's peaceful
rayons.”

Russia: Chernomyrdin: ‘Carrot-and-Stick Policy’
To Continue in Chechnya

LD2304171196 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1552 GMT 23 Apr 96

(From the “Presidential Bulletin” feature]

(FBIS Transcribed Text] [no dateline as received] A
working commission formed by President Boris Yeltsin
flies out to Groznyy on Wednesday to investigate last
week's ambush of a Russian convoy by Chechen rebels
in the south which, according to varying counts, left
from 53 to 90 Russian troops dead, Prime Minister Vik-
tor Chernomyrdin told a session of the state commission
for Chechnya he leads on Tuesday.

He said the working commission is expected to report
back to the president by next Tuesday.

The prime minister said there were restrictions set on
Russian troops in fighting against terrorism in Chechnya
and that “no one has ever talked about this.” In this
connection, he said the U.N. charter provides for what
is called coercion to peace.

He reaffirmed that Moscow would continue a carrot-
and-stick policy in Chechnya.

On the prospects of a regional peace settlement, Cher-
nomyrdin said the rebel attack on Russian troops out-
side Dachu-Borzoy showed that “the bandits do not want
peace.”

Pre enn eaoed maneiak Coping cod Saseninction
is prohibited without permission of the copynght owners.

FBIS-SOV -96-080
24 April 1996

On the positive side, he pointed to a peace pact drawn
up by representatives of the peoples of Chechnya at
their assembly in Groznyy last week. The pact is open
for signing by all peace upholders in the republic.

Commission members and Russian Nationalities Min-
ister Vyacheslav Mikhaylov involved in the assembly
session believe that most Chechens want peace, Cher-
nomyrdin said.

In the meantime, the Russian State Duma is preparing
a resolution on amnesty to members of all rebel units
in Chechnya except those involved in grievous crimes,
Duma Speaker Gennadiy Seleznev told Interfax on
Tuesday.

President Yeitsin’s peace plan of March 31 also held
out the possibility of amnesty including separatist leader
Dzhokhar Dudayev.

Seleznev said the Yeltsin plan would come under
house discussion at a session in May. He said the
delay is caused by a lack of mechanism of appropriate
discussion. “This could be a report by Chernomyrdin
as head of the commission for Chechnya or republican
head Doku Zavgayev or any commission member from
the government side,” he said expressing certainty that
the issue would be discussed next month.

Seleznev is against publication of the details of the
Yeltsin plan because of the existence of powerful groups
opposed to the plan as evidenced by the rebel attack on a
Russian convoy on April 16. “If we draw up and publish
the schedules of troop withdrawals from Chechnya, we
will have prepared the rebels in advance,” he believes.

For his part, Deputy Duma Speaker Alexander Shokhin
thinks the details of the plan must be made available
only to a narrow circle of officials.

In an interview with Interfax on Monday, he said this
includes leaders of the parliamentary factions and heads
of the related committees — defense and security.

He said the peace plan must contain details such as
kinds of troops, army actions, definition of a sanitary
<ordon around Chechnya and prevention of arms supply
or infiltration of militants from neighboring Dagestan,
Azerbaijan and Ingushetia.

Shokhin blamed the Dachu-Borzoy tragedy on the
absence of such a plan. He also believes that any
involvement in drafting a peace plan by the leaders of
Duma factions would indicate that concerted effort is
being taken to resolve a painful issue while “forgetting
that this can be a topic for political demonstrations
ahead of the presidential election.”

In the meantime, the Parliamentary Assembly of the
Council of Europe, PACE, has called for an immediate

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 47

implementation of the Yeltsin plan or any other peace
plans. But it said no progress could be made unless all
parties including Dudayev are involved.

This follows a draft resolution by the Assembly's
political commission to be unveiled at the PACE session
on Wednesday.

The resolution calls for an unconditional condemnation
of human rights abuses in Chechnya both by federal
troops and Chechen rebels.

It accuses Russian troops of disproportionate and unjus-
tified use of force against civilians banned by the 1949
Geneva convention and the 1977 second protocol to it.

The resolution says Russia has so far failed to honor
obligations towards Chechnya it gave while entering
into the Council of Europe.

It proposes that PACE’s special commission for Chech-
nya meet urgently in Moscow and Groznyy to help
the OSCE mission restart dialogue between the warring
sides.

Russia: Grachev Says Statements on Chechen

[By Andrey Shtorkh]

{[FBIS Transcribed Text} KHABAROVSK, April 23
(Itar-Tass) — Russian Defence Minister Pavel Grachev
said on Tuesday that the federal force in Chechnya
is complying with the cessation of military operations
ordered by President Boris Yeltsin on March 31.

The compliance is completely relevant to the situation
in which the federal troops are, Grachev said.

Speaking to reporters at the Khabarovsk airport today,
Grachev explained that the presidential decree to stop
military operations at midnight March 31 immediately
reached the troops as the order to move back.

However, the troops required six days to go out of action
with minimal losses.

“After six days, complying with the president's decree,
we have gone out of combat, moved away from
the adversary and deployed in base areas. After that
preparation has been started for moving part of units
to permanent deployment sites outside the territory of
Chechnya,” Grachev said.

As for the 205th Motorised Infantry Division, which
is to remain in Chechnya for permanent deployment,
Grachev said it “now is in its positions at permanent
deployment sites in Shali and at two airports — Sev-
erniy «nd Khankala.”

Reporters asked for Grachev's clarifications about the
criticism of the presidential cease-fire decree which he
voiced in the State Duma on April 19 and about his
earlier statement that the orders reached troops only on
April 6.
