{[FBIS Translated Text] The Communists are moving
their heavy artillery into position. At the last plenary
meeting of the Communist Party of Kazakhstan on
13 April, Serikboisyn Abdildin, ex-speaker of the self-
dissolved Supreme Soviet, chairman of the Respublika
coordination council, and Doctor of Economics, was
elected first secretary of the Central Committee of the
Communist Party of Kazakhstan. Mr. Abdildin gave an
exclusive interview to DELOVAYA NEDELYA.

[DELOVAYA NEDEL YA] Serikboisyn Abdildayevich,
what is your attitude toward the Communist Party of
Russia and its leader Gennadiy Zyuganov? How do you
assess his chances of victory [in the Russian presidential
election} on 16 June?

{Abdildin} My attitude toward the Communist Party of
Russia is, on the whole, positive. I am not personally
acquainted with Gennadiy Andreyevich ({Zyuganov],
although we met when I was working in Moscow. I am
closely watching his activities now, and | think he is
an honest man. This is the primary quality for a leader.
Zyuganov is a Doctor of Philosophy, is popular among

CENTRAL ASIA 71

the masses, and is running for president in a bloc that
millions of people support.

As a rule, the opponents of the leader of the Russian
communists try to scare voters with a return to a
totalitarian regime if he wins. In my opinion, it is
not fair, as Zyuganov has nothing in common with
the [Soviet] regime: he is for a multi-party system,
multi-structural ownership, and a law-governed .iate.
Totalitarism is, above all, characteristic of the present
president [Boris Yeltsin], who was first secretary of the
[Sverdlovsk] regional party committee, first secretary
of Moscow city party committee, and a candidate-
member of the Politburo of the Central Committee of
the Commuanist Party of the Soviet Union.

If the people of Russia are fighting for justice in the real
meaning of the word, then Zyuganov should be elected,
maybe even in the first round. Elections are a sort of
battle between good and evil, and unfortunately reason
does not always triumph, but this time the people of
Russia have a chance to sort out who is who from their
Own experience.

[DELOVAYA NEDELYA| Serikbolsyn Abdildayevich,
what do the ideals of communism mean to you person-
ally?

[Abdildin}] For me they have specific meanings, if we
bear in mind that communist ideals include the funda-
mental human standards, which the popular masses have
been developing for thousands of years. They include
humane relations and mutual respect between people,
honesty, truthfulness, moral purity, simplicity, modesty
in general human and individual life, concern for the
upbringing of children, implacable opposition to injus-
tice, and so on. Do our people not need these qualities
today?

Second, communism is a scientific theory — a theory,
not a dogma. Therefore the development of science must
be based not only on ideals, but also on real experience.

[DELOVAYA NEDELYA] Does your election as first
secretary of the Central Commitice of the Communist
Party of Kazakhstan mean a change to the party's
economic program?

[Abdildin}] A change to the party program's economic
policy is quite possible, taking into account the real
situation in society. This is an issue for our next
congress.

[DELOVAYA NEDEL YA] Serikboisyn Abdildayevich,
why do you think that the (CIS) integration declared in
words is virtually not being implemented in deeds”

[Abdildin] There are objective and subjective reasons
for that. One of the reasons, in Kazakhstan's experience,

72 CENTRAL ASIA

is that the executive branch of power proclaimed the
self-regulation of the non-existent market with the aim
of forming a clan-national bourgeoisie. A governable

Unfortunately, the present power-that-be have been

Tajikistan: Russian Paper Views ‘Unprecedented’
Economic Crisis

MM2304152896 Moscow TRUD in Russian

23 Apr 96 p4

[Report by Anatoliy Larenok: “Tajikistan. Recession"]

{[FBIS Translated Text] The economic crisis in Tajik-
istan is unprecedented in its scale. A predominantly
agrarian country, the republic reduced agricultural pro-
duction by as much as another 20 percent last year. The
production of raw cotton proved to be 333,000 tonnes
less than planned, for grain the figure was 370,000, and
potatoes — 92,000 tonnes. There are great losses in
livestock farming.

According to foreign experts, the main reason for the
crisis is that reforms in the agrarian sector are faltering.
There is low production efficiency on the collective and
state farms, which do not have economic freedom.

Power generation has declined. Consumers received
only 49.1 percent of the thermal energy needed — which
had a negative effect on industrial enterprises’ operation.

CENTRAL ASIA 73

The volume of aluminum production, for example, has
fallen to less than one-half in recent years.

The volume of capital investments has fallen by 60
percent. Construction materials production is practically
paralyzed.

The country’s transportation system is on the brink of
complete collapse. Over half of public transport vehicles
are standing idle. There is no money to restore and repair
railroad transportation.

Today all you can buy with a minimum wage in
Tajikistan is 1.5 kg of potatoes, and with a minimum
pension — three bread rolls. The population's income
has fallen by almost 60 percent. “The living standard in
the country is so low,” trade union experts are noting,
“that it does not even guarantee the physicai survival
of the majority of the population.” Unemployment
is growing. The drugs business is spreading. A few
days ago, border guards seized over 35 kg of narcotic
substances in the Pamir in a 24-hour period alone, and
the figure is 800 kg since the beginning of the year.

Analysts from the International Foundation for the
Support of Political and Economic Reforms believe that
in order to get out of the protracted crisis it is necessary
first and foremost to settle the inter-Tajik conflict on the
basis of a political compromise, and then implement a
program for the denationalization of the economy, pay
off wage arrears by issuing privatization checks to the
population, giving them the right to own shares in state
property...

When and how such reasonable recommendations will
be implemented is hard to say at the moment.

CAUCASUS 75

prehensive resolution to the problem.

the resolution of the conflict will significantly contribute
to the stability and security of the region, and to the
economic and social development of the people.

speedy resolution of the humanitarian issues. The need
for swift and unconditional release of hostages and
prisoners of war was stressed above all [word indistinct).

Presidents Ter-Petrosyan and Aliyev agreed to continue
their contacts.

Armenia: Liparityan: No Documents Signed
Without Karabakh

NC2204141596 Yerevan ARAGIL ELECTRONIC
NEWS BULLETIN in English 1111 GMT 22 Apr 96

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Yerevan (“Respublica Arme-
nia” daily), 04/22/96 — Chief Advisor of the ROA
{Republic of Armenia] President Zhirayr Liparityan re-
turned from Ankara on 13 April. Armenian media have
already quoted Turkish CUMHURIYET, MILLIYET,
and Marmara’s publications in this regard. RESPUB-
LICA ARMENIA now published an exclusive interview
with presidential advisor, some extracts from which we
publish below.

— Our society is mostly interested in the results of
your trip to Turkey, and whether there is any progress
in Armenia-Turkey relations.

— Interest in regard with our meetings with Turkish
leaders is understandable. During a mecting with Prime
Minister Yilmaz we touched upon his declaration re-
garding Armenian-Turkish relations, when he said that
Turkey is ready to open its borders if a document about
principally important problems is signed.

— You mean the problem of resolution of the Karabakh
conflict?

— Yes. naturaily, in regard with the problem of
Mountainous Karabakh. We can see that there are some
changes in the position of Turkey in this regard. Turkish

This report may contain copynghted maternal. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copynght owners

76 CAUCASUS

side insisted that the whole document shall be signed,
but we advised that it is not necessary to relate two
problems with each other.

We believe that if a solution is found to the Karabakh
conflict, which satisfies all the sides, then this document
will be signed by Armenia, regardless its consequences.

— But do you think that Armenia, based on certain
considerations, can possibly sign the document without
Mountainous Karabakh's participation?

— Minsk group is dealing with preparation of the
Grand Political Agreement. And I can say for sure that
Armenia will not sign any poliical agreement without
participation of Mountainous Karabakh. This principle
remains unchanged.

The last report of Minsk group to the UN Security Coun-
cil states clearly that the agreement should be signed by
three sides of the conflict: Armenia, Azerbaijan, and
Mowntainous Karabakh. But the process of resolution
on the level of Minsk group is too slow, and proposals
are being made to sign a primary documents about key
points of resolution.

We do not relate the jcoblem of resolution of the
Karabakh conflict with Armenia-Turkey relations. We
realize though that the problem, say, of Turkey's
blockade of Armenia is rather significant for Azerbaijan.
Replying to Turkish Prime Minister, President Levon
Ter-Petrosyan stressed that Turkey should seriously
think about the consequences of such pressure on
the process of negotiations, and to what extent will
it facilitate the process of normalization of Turkish-
Armenian relations. This problem was central during
our conversation with Turkish Prime Minister.

— Armenian media quoted your words that “even if
Turkey does not unlock the borders for another 1,000
years..."

— Yes, during the meeting with Turkish Envoy [Ayhan]
Kamel we said directly: even if Turkey declares that it
will keep its borders locked for another 1,000 years, we
won't sign a political document, which is not accept-
able for the Armenian side. So, we won't accept any
compromises if they endanger Mountainous Karabakh's
security, regardless whether it concerns the problem of
gas pipe-line or unlocking the borders.

— Armenian press has been often accusing the leaders
of Armenia in forgetting the history, particularly in
regard with the genocide of 1915...

— The position of Armenian government in regard
with the genocide remains the same. Best of all, it was
expressed in President Levon Ter-Petrosyan's speech at

the international conference dedicated to the genocide
last year.

— Do you think that the position of Turkey in this
regard may improve?

— Yes, I think this is possible.

Cooperation

LD2404105996 Moscow INTERFAX in English

[FBIS Transcribed Text] YEREVAN, Apri! 24 (Inter-
fax) — Armenian Industrial Minister Amot Safaryan
and the executive director of the Iranian government
organization for social well-being Mehdi Karbasiyan
signed a memorandum on mutual understanding be-
tween the two countries and a packet of bilateral eco-
nomic agreements.

Iran and Armenia plan to create a wide range of joint
ventures in the oil refining, chemical, pharmaceutical,
and food products areas, and bilateral integration is also
planned in light industry, director of Razdanmash and
co-chairman of the intergovernmental working group
Telman Ter-Petrosyan said. Armenia is counting on a
large flow of Iranian investment to create a complex
base of joint production. On the other side, Armenian
producers have received many opportunities to enter the
iranian market.

Mutually beneficial cooperation with Iran “will not get
in the way of closer ties with the CIS, specifically
Armenia’s possible entrance into the CIS Customs
Union,” Safaryan said.

Iran is the second largest investor in the Armenian
economy, the Iranian Melab bank has opened a branch
in Armenia, and a number of joint ventures and tourist
companies are already in operation.

22, the press service of the Armenian president told
Interfax Tuesday.

signed agreements on partnership and <:ooperation with

the talks in the framework of the OSCE [Organization

on Nagorno-Karabakh, and the activization of a direct
dialogue between the parties in order to ensure the
signing of a political agreement, according to the press
service.

The presidents also agreed that the conflict’s settlement
would be a substantial contribution to the establishment
of stability and security in the region and would promote
the economic and social development of peoples living
in the Transcaucasus.

The leaders of the two countries expressed a common
opinion on the need to solve humanitarian issues as soon
as possible, primarily, an immediate and unconditional
liberation of all hostages and POWs, the presidential
press service told Interfax.

Complexes ‘Buk-M1"" — TURAN headline]

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Baku/22.04.96/TURAN —
Russia and Armenia are going to sign an agreement
for delivery of some anti-aireraft complex “Buk-M1",
according to sources in the head-quarter of Caucasian
Special Border District of Russia. Military specialists
consider that this system of anti-air defence is better
than the system “Tunguska” and “Shilka” which are
presently available in Russian and Armenian military
groups.

According to informed sources, even in 1994 two
systems “Shilka” of Russian forces in Armenia were
transferred to the southern part of the Karabakh front
(Fizuli region of Azerbaijan.)

If new anti-air defence system “Buk-M1" are transferred
to the Armenian-Azeri front the ability the Armenian
anti-air defence system to strike the targets at the terri-
tory of Azerbaijan will grow, military experts consider.

It is noteworthy that the negotiations about deliveries to
Armenia of more modern systems are taking place after
the next refusal of Azerbaijan to join the united anti-air
defence system of the CIS.

Reinforcing of the Armenian forces is assessed by
military specialists as one more pressure of Moscow
on Azerbaijan. Russia indirectly pushes Armenia and
separatists in Nagorno-Karabakh to further withstanding
with Azerbaijan through increasing the military power
of Armenia and by this disturbing the balance of forces
and means in the region.

Azerbaijan: Matalibov, Gaziyev Case Not Linked
to Russian Relations

LD2204173296 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1603 GMT 22 Apr 96

[From the “Presidential Bulletin” feature]

[FBIS Transcribed Text} President Geydar Aliyev cate-
gorically disagrees with the assertion that the arrest of

FBIS-SOV-96-080
24 April 1996

ex-president Ayaz Mutalibov and the extradition of ex-
defense minister Ragim Gaziyev were “Moscow's re-
sponse to Baku’s concessions.”

“These are primitive insinuations,” he told reporters in
the Baku airport shortly before his departure to Brussels,
where the Azerbaijani, Armenian and Georgian leaders
are to sign a partnership and cooperation agreement with
the European Union Monday.

He said he had not failed to notice such judgements in
the Moscow press and was “greatly surprised by them.”

He also said inter-state relations cannot be influenced
by private factors, particularly relations between Azer-
baijan and “such a great country as Russia.”

He emphasized that Azerbaijan viewed Russia as a
friendly country, cooperation with which has nothing to
do with “momentary considerations” and which does not
depend on whether or not a criminal will be extradited.

“Otherwise, the level of relations between the two
countries would be lowered,” he said.

“Russia and Azerbaijan are above these private inci-
dents,” he said referring to the arrest of Ayaz Mutalibov
and the extradition of Ragim Gaziyev. In his opinion,
these incidents point to nothing else but “good cooper-
ation between the two countries’ law-enforcement bod-
ies.”

IF Note: Ragim Gaziyev, who, with four high-ranking
Azerbaijani military officials, was accused of surren-
dering to Armenia regional centers Shusha and Lachin
in the zone of the Karabakh conflict, escaped from the
preliminary detention prison of the Azerbaijani Interior
Ministry on September 22. Gaziyev is also faced with
charges of organizing a state coup in Azerbaijan in Oc-
tober 1994 and March 1995.

Ayaz Mutalibov, who was taken to a Moscow hospital
with a heart attack following his arrest, iias been accused
by Baku of organizing mass disturbances and attempted
seizure of power. He is also faced with charges of
“connivance and inactivity” during the introduction of
Soviet troops to Baku in January 1990, when, according
to official sources, more than 130 people were killed,
700 wounded and 40 reported missing.

In the opinion of Azerbaijan's current authorities,
through Mutalibov’s fault Azerbaijani villages were
surrendered to the Armenian formations in the zone of
the Karabakh conflict in the spring of 1992.

At the same time, Aliyev praised “active and very suc-
cessful cooperation” between Azerbaijan and Russia, in-
cluding within the framework of the CIS. As an exam-
ple he referred to the projects on the joint operation

This report may contain copynghted matenal Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copynght owners

FBIS-SOV -96-080
24 April 1996

of off-shore Caspian oil fields. He said that the Rus-
sian company LUKoil plays an important role in the
recently established international consortium for imple-
menting “an oil contract of the century.” He said that
in January 1996 he and Russian Prime Minister Viktor
Chernomyrdin signed an agreement on the transporta-
tion of early oil from Azerbaijan through Russia to the
Black Sea port of Novorossiysk.

Aliyev also emphasized Baku's determination to do
everything in its power to broaden relations with Russia
in other areas.

He said that agreement on bilateral cooperation had a
“large-scale and inter-state character”. Compared to this,
“the personalities of Gaziyev and Mutalibov are a mere
trifle,” he said.

He said that if Gaziyev and Mutalibov plead guilty, the
Azerbaijani authorities will mitigate the punishment.

“Our laws are just. If a person has regrets about what
he has done, he may always count on mercy,” he said.

However, in any case, Mutalibov “must return to
Azerbaijan and be tried,” said Aliyev.

He gave to understand that the charges brough' against
Mutalibov do not incur capital punishment.

“People must not leave their homeland even if they have
committed a crime,” he said. “It would be better to clear
the issue with the people and with the authorities than
to seek refuge in some country,” he said.

FBIS-SOV-96-080
24 April 1996

uard Shevardnadze said after he met NATO Secretary-
General Javier Solana on Tuesday.

“Nobody can impose any restrictions on NATO's en-
largement, however it (the enlargement) should not re-
sult in a new confrontation in Europe or the entire
world,” Shevardnadze said.

“I think a way can be find to make the enlargement
painless. Many things will depend on the promotion
of cooperation between NATO and Russia. But on the
whole, each country makes its own choice,” he said.

In a comment on recent statements by Vladislav Ardz-
inba, leader of Georgia's independence-seeking republic
of Abkhazia, that Abkhazia may agree only to a “feder-
ation union” with Georgia and not to autonomy status,
the Georgian leader said that talks were actively going
on through Russian mediation and under the auspices of
the United Nations.

“We offer Abkhazia the maximum amount of autonomy,
a constitution, a parliament, a hymn, a flag, and more
independence in solving economic issues,” he said.

“I consider compromise is possible on such a basis
and with active mediation by Russia, the U.N., and,
hopefully, European bodies,” he added.

Shevardnadze said his visit to the NATO Headquarters
was a logical continuation of his visit to the European
Union Headquarters where he had signed cooperation
aad partnership accords between Georgia and the EU.

The Georgian president said Solana, an old friend of
his, had accepted an invitation to pay an official visit
to Thilisi. Solana, in turn, said he would use the
Opportunity in the nearest future.

Georgia: Tbilisi Blast Death Toll Reaches Five;
Sabotage Ruled Out

LD2404104096 Tbilisi IPRINDA in Russian
0900 GMT 24 Apr 96

(FBIS Translated Text} Tbilisi, 24 Apr — An IPRINDA
correspondent has been told at the press center of
the Georgian Interior Ministry that as a result of the
explosion in Tbilisi on 23 April, five people have now
died and 42 are in hospital with various degrees of
injury.

Preliminary investigation has shown that the explosion
was not a terrorist attack or an act of sabotage, but was
caused by a gas leak in the basement of the shop where
the explosion happened.

Estonia: Tallinn Rejects Russia’s Accusations of
Discrimination

WS1904135496 Tallinn ETA in English
1305 GMT 19 Apr 96

["News Release"]

{FBIS Transcribed Text} Tallinn, April 19 (ETA) —
Russian delegation reiterated to the UN human rights
comittee in Geneva on Friday that the Russian-
spe*¥ing part of the population in Estonia and Latvia
is being discriminated.

Estonian Foreign Ministry spokesman Juri Arusoo said
in response that such accusations were pari of Russia's
official policy towards Estonia.

The accusations addressed to Estonia have not fulfilled
their purpose and have been proved ungrounded, Arusoo
said.

"In response to Russia's propaganda experts from nu-
merous international organisations have visited Estonia
to get acquainted with the situation. They have asserted
that Estonia corresponds to the norms of international
justice and that the rights of ethnic minorities are not
infringed upon,” Arusoo told ETA.

Arusoo went on to say that the accusations are based
on Russia’s official policy which regards Estonia not
as a country which regaine independence after an
occupation of 50 years but as a country which was
formed as a result of the break-up of the Soviet Union.

Leader of the Russian delegation Boris Krylov said in
Geneva that the discriminatory policies against Russian-
Speaking minorities in Estonia and Latvia have to ve
brought to an end.

He paid special attention to the Orthodox Church row in
Estonia, saying the Russian Orthodox congregations are
being harassed in Estonia now that the Constantinople
Mother Church has officially recognised the Estonian
Orthodox Church.

Krylov called upon international organisations and for-
eign powers to use their influence and authority for find-
ing a just solution to the problem.

Estonian Foreign Ministry holds a close eye on Russia's
Statements and reacts to them in the UN human rights
committee in due time, Arusoo told ETA.

Estonia: UN Human Rights Commission Denied
Anti-Estonian Resolution

WS2204092096 Tallinn BNS in English
1836 GMT 19 Apr 96

(FBIS Transcribed Text} Tallinn, Apr 19, BNS — The
U.N. Human Rights Commission at its spring session
did not back Russia's accusations against Estonia, Heino
Ainso, a member of the Estonian delegation to the
commission, told BNS.

“Russia's aim was to have an anti-Estonian resolution
or at least a comment from the commission chairman,
but its attempts fell through,” Ainso said.

In his words, Russian representatives at the commis-
sion’s meeting in Geneva hinted more than once at
abuse of human rights in Estonia and also delivered
a fierce accusatory speech. “But, not a single state sup-
ported them,” he said.

Russia's last attempt to raise the Estonian question
was on Thursday when it was supposed to explain the
situation in Chechnya.

A Russian delegate said an end had to be put to the
discrimination of the Russian minority ‘tn Latvia and
especially in Estonia, where Orthodox believers were
being oppressed, Ainso explained.

Russia called on the states that had sought the with-
drawal of Russian troops from the Baltic countries for
help in finding a just solution to the human rights prob-
lem, but this was treated as an attempt to divert attention
from the Chechen issue, the Estonian delegate said.

The Human R.ghts Commission comprises represenia-
tives from 53 U.N. member nations. Its spring session
lasts from March 19 through Apr. 27.

Latvia: Norwegian Premier, Ulmanis Comment on
NATO, Security

WS2304133796 Tallinn BNS in English
0919 GMT 23 Apr 96

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Riga, Apr 23, BNS — NATO
is an Open organization and no one other than its mem-
ber states will decide on the admission of new members,
Norwegian Prime Minister Gro Harlem Brundtland told
the Latvian president Monday.

However, attempts should be made to enchance safety
by economic and political contacts as military coopera-
tion should not be viewed as a priority, the president's
office quoted Brundtland as saying.

Security in Europe is impossible without cooperation
with neighboring countries as “the still existent military
threat does not allow one to feel entirely safe,” Brundt-
land told Ulmanis.

Ulmanis voiced his opinion that the competition princi-
ple should not be applied in the process of enlargement
of international organizations.

“I do not support the spirit of competition with regard
to the admission of (...) countries to international bodies
because I consider that a common European security
policy is more important,” he said.

Latvia: Birkavs Interviewed on Security, Baltic
Cooperation

LD2204170496 Riga Raaw Riga Network in Latvian
1300 GMT 22 Apr 96

{Interv’.:w with Latvian Foreign Minister Valdis Birkavs
by un Jentified correspondent; place and date not given
— live or recorded]

(FBIS Translated Text} [Correspondent] NATO Secre-
tary General Javier Solana visited Latvia last week and
it is now clear NATO will be enlarged, but when will
it happen? Neither is it known yet whether Latvia will
be a member of NATO. Last week, we could also read
in the press that unofficial Russian opinions on foreign
policy deem it possible that Russia could militarily in-
tervene in Latvia's internal affairs if it joined NATO. Is

BALTIC STATES 87

the Foreign Ministry currently considering any parallel
security guarantees while it is on its way to NATO?

(Birkavs} Yes, in any case it is completely clear that
the only true security guarantee is full membership
of NATO. What do parallel security structures mean?
Parallel security structures mean that without changing
our aim of joining NATO, we work very hard on Baltic
cooperation — Baltic military cooperation and we work
very hard in the framework of bilateral agreements.
For example, Norwegian Prime Minister Brundtland
is currently visiting I atvia and of course we will
also have talks with her on security issues, because
we have already quite developed cooperation with the
Norwegian Defense Ministry. The same applies to other
countries that are our neighbors looking toward the
West. However, irrespective of this, only NATO can
provide guarantees to Latvia.

{Correspondent} History has proven, however, that it
has never been [word indistinct] for the Baltic states to
cooperate [words indistinct] due to various considera-
tions. How do you assess at the moment the possibility
for all three Baltic states to really cooperate in the mil-
itary sphere, and not only in the military sphere?

[Birkavs] There have been two periods in our history
—- one was a period of bad cooperation and the second
is a period of » 9wing cooperation. The period of bad
cooperation was before World War II and the period of
growing Cooperation is at present. Yes, we are stronger
together, but those are not our security guaraiitees either.

[Correspondent] How do you assess yesterday's meeting
with John Mroz, the president of the Institute of East
West Studies?

[Birkavs} Very positively, because we agreed on three
things. We agreed on active cooperation at the level
of thinking itself, i.e. at the level of political influence,
because they are already working on how the world
will look like, how they could influence it, and how
to balance interests. We will work in three directions;
one is NATO, which you have already mentioned, the
second is Latvia and the European Union — we agreed
on a broad conference in Latvia — and the third is
economic cooperation, but in that respect that they
are experts, they have a special emphasis on banking
and finance sector. This cooperation will be developing
within the framework of the Baltic Initiative Fund that
will be founded after the presidents of the three Baltic
States will be given an annual award, because the award
ceremony is also a money raising campaign for the
Baltic Initiative Fund that will begin its independent
life after the award ceremony

Latvia: President Expresses Concerns to UN on
Refugee Situation

LD2004101696 Riga Radio Riga Network in Latvian
1700 GMT 19 Apr 96

{[FBIS Translated Text] Europe must definitely reckon
on a new wave of refugees, which will also affect
Latvia, if conflicts such as that in the Caucasus are not
resolved peacefully, the state president of Latvia be-
lieves. Guntis Ulmanis expressed this view in Geneva to
Sadako Ogata, the UN high commissioner for refugees.
To forestall such a possibility, Ulmanis stressed the need
for the UN to take action. If individual countries are
unable to solve their national problems, t.ey become
global. Ulmanis expressed the conviction that the 150
refugees currently housed in Latvia are not a problem
of national significance.

Latvia: New Round of Russian-Latvian Border
Talks Begin in Riga

LD1904171996 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1513 GMT 19 Apr 9

{[FBIS Transcribed Text! RIGA, April 19 (Interfax) —
Latvia and Russia started a fresh round of border talks
on Friday, chief Russian negotiator Viktor Shikalov told
Interfax.

This follows Moscow's rejection of negotiations the
day before on the grounds that Riga’s position was
unacceptable.

Riga insists that the talks must be based on the
1920 peace treaty ceding an area of 1,640 square
kilometers around the town of Pytalovo to Latvia. It
has a population of 16,000, of which ethnic Latvians
make up a mere 0.1 percent.

The Soviet government brought the region under Rus-
sia’s jurisdiction in 1944,

Shikalov said the Latvians handed the Russian team a
note on Thursday proposing restoration of the common
border between the two countries. “The Russian side
believes that such an agenda would imply Latvian
territorial claims on Russia,” he said.

But he «aid common sense prevailed on Friday after
Riga agreed to delete the word “restore.”

Shikalov ssid tha in Moscow's view, the 1920 treaty
had become invalid after Latvia's incorporation into the
Soviet Union in 1940. “The Russian Federation and
other countries recognized the Latvian Republic as it
was in 1991,” he said.

Latvian Foreign Minister Valdis Birkavs, speaking on
national television on Thursday, said although Moscow
dismisses the fact that the Latvian Republic existed

FBIS-SOV -96-080
24 April 1996

before the Second World War, Riga was prepared to
continue what he called “constructive talks.”

The next round of talks is due in Moscow later next
month.

Lithuania: Prime Minister, Polish Counterpart on
Regional Cooperation

LD2004152596 Moscow INTERFAX in English

1233 GMT 20 Apr 96

(FBIS Transcribed Text) VILNIUS, Apr 20 (Interfax)
— It is expedient to begin to form a new joint struc-
ture — Neman Euroregion — for more effective co-
operation between Poland, Lithuania, Belarus and Rus-
sia’s Kaliningrad Region, said Poland's and Lithuania’s
Prime Ministers Wlodzimierz Cimoszewicz and Min-
daugas Stankevicius at their talks held on Saturday in
the Polish town of Augustow on Lithuania's border.

The new structure of regional cooperation will step up
interaction between the four countrics and make it easier
for them to accomplish joint economic, ecological and
transport projects, the press service of the Lithuanian
government told Interfax.

According to it, during their working mecting the prime
ministers of Lithuania and Poland decided to sign an
intergovernmental agreement on free trade during the
Official visit to be paid by Stankevicius to Warsaw in
June.

When the Lithuanian prime minister called for opening
more check points on their border Cimoszewicz said
that Poland's budget for this year does not provide for
allocating funds required for these needs

The two prime ministers confirmed that integration
into NATO and other European structures is their
top priority. Besides, Poland will give Lithuania aid
in its efforts to join the Central Europe Free Trade
Organization.

Lithuania: Czech President Declares ‘Solidarity’
With Vilnius on EU, NATO

W3/904134896 Tallinn BNS in English
1300 GMT 19 Apr 96

{[FBIS Transcribed Text} Vilnius, Apr 19, BNS
Czech President Vaclav Havel made a long-anticipated
move in Vilnius Thursday [18 April] by declaring sol-
idarity with Lithuania in its strive to join the European
Union and NATO

"We share the same major foreign policy priorities: to
become NATO and EU members,” Have! said at a port

This report may contain copynghted matenal Copying and dissemination
1s prohibited without permission of the copynght owners

FBIS-SOV -96-080
24 April 1996

news conference with Lithuanian President Algirdas
Brazauskas.

The two countries can only reach those aims by way of
mutual cooperation. “If it is turned into a race, our goals
will become more difficult to achieve", Havel said.

Brazauskas ‘oted with satisfaction that the two heads
of state shared the stance that equal starting conditions
should be offered to all Central European candidates for
NATO and EU membership.

“It is important that the countries had the condition set
that negotiations begin at the same time,” the Lithuanian
president sa:o.

Havel said preparations by Lithuania to sign a free trade
agreement with the Czech Republic will open the door
for Lithuania to the CEFTA (Central European Free
Trade Association) market.

Both heads of state stressed that democratic develop-
ment in Russia is important to all of Europe and were
critical of the Russian Duma decision to declare null
and void the agreements that sealed the breakup of the
Soviet Union.

“Lithuania is not affected, since the Soviet Union
recognized its independence before it was dissolved,”
Brazauskas said.

Havel, in turn, said he doesn't attribute any significance
to the Duma decision as the Soviet Union no longer
exists. “This is the same thing if the Austrian parliament
ruled to announce that the collapse of the Austrian-
Hungarian empire was illegal,” he said.

During his visit to Lithuania, Havel also met with the
leader of the right-wing opposition, Vytautus Landsber-
gis. The Czech president said he had got the impres-
sion that the government and the opposition had similar
views on all most important foreign policy issues.

Commenting on a large protest demonstration that took
place near the parliament seat and the president's office
at the time of his meetings in Vilnius Thursday, Havel
said it would seem something was wrong if there were
no such demonstrations in a democratic government.

Because of the rally, in which some 5,000 people
protested against economic hardships, the joint press
conference of the Lithuanian and Czech presidents was
transfered from Brazauskas’ office to the government
guest house, and the formal farewell — to the airport.

Lithuania: Vilnius To Keep Lithuanian
Peacekeepers in Bosnia

LD1904194996 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1709 GMT 19 Apr 96

[From the “Diplomatic Panorama” feature}

{[FBIS Transcribed Text} VILNIUS, April 19 (Interfax)
— Lithuania will keep its international commitments
ON maintaining peace under the aegis of NATO and
contribute to rebuilding Bosnia, says a statement of
the country’s Foreign Ministry on the death of a
Lithuanian officer and the wounding of two Lithuanian
peacekeepers in Bosnia.

The ministry expressed condolences to the family and
loved ones of the dead man and sympathy with the
wounded men. “The death of a Lithuanian officer
and a Danish serviceman in Bosnia is a tragic and
symbolic confirmation of the integrity and indivisibility
of European security. These men died for peace in the
whole of Europe as well as Bosnia and, consequently
in Lithuania,” the statement says.

Lithuanian President Algirdas Brazauskas awarded a
medal posthumously to Normundas Valteris.

At a joint news conference Brazauskas and his Czech
counterpart Vaclav Havel expressed regret over the
death of a Lithuanian peacekeeper.

Lukashenka.

Lithuania: Border Problems, Talks Detailed

WS1804131296 Tallinn BNS in English
1300 GMT 17 Apr 96

{(FBIS Transcribed Text) Vilnius, Apr 17, BNS —
Lithuania says biggest unresolved problems in bor-
der affairs remain with Belarus, while Russian border
guards themseives will come to Vilnius for talks.

The main problem is the inflow of illegal Asian immi-
grants and Belarus's unwillingness io take them back.

Grazvydas Bacevicius, adviser to the head of the Lithua-
nian border authority, said he didn’t think the Belarusian
border guards had unilaterally broken off contacts with
Lithuania when they kept postponing a meeting due in
their capital.

“Whea the weather gets warm, they themselves have
loads of problems with economic immigrants and per-
haps they really have not found time to meet with us,”
the official said.

Border leaders from both countries meet regularly, and
according to the order established it is now Belarus's

FBIS-SOV-96-080
24 April 1996

turn to invite the Lithuanian colleagues. The meeting
was supposed to take place at the beginning of April,
but until now the Lithuanian border police department
has neither received an invitation nor explanation.

At the same time the Kaliningrad region border authori-
ties will come to Vilnius to solve problems with Lithua-
nia. Anatoliy Rachmanin, commander of the Kalin-
ingrad region border troops, will head a delegation to
Vilnius April 23-24.

It is planned to negotiate with the Russian representa-
tives on joint actions during the coming tourist season.
Many Germans would like to make the journey from the
Kursiu spit to Kaliningrad, and for them certain easing
of requirements in crossing the border may be made.

Kaliningrad and Lithuanian border guards will also
negotiate on problems arising from unmarked state
borders.

Lithuanian border police department director Stanislo-
vas Stancikas and Rachmanin are expected to sign a
cooperation plan.
